# Infinite Realms — Phase 1 cleanup audit

**Review only. No cleanup is authorized by this report.** This PR contains this Markdown file only. No application code, tests, migrations, prompts, assets, branches, or production data were changed or deleted. Implementation is a separate agreement, item by item. Do not merge — for review.

Audited on **2026-09-09**, against `main` commit `ab716f53be7ae8a6de187f60b827e242ceb9991f` (release 0.15.0). Source links are pinned to that commit. GitHub inventory: 388 current remote branches, 1,834 PR records, and three open PRs, captured before this audit PR. The local clone is not shallow. An isolated worktree was used; pre-existing shared-checkout edits were preserved.

The eight requested areas appear below in review-priority order, with their original area numbers. Within each area, findings with greater consequences come first. “Safe” describes the proposed change after approval, not permission to act now.

- **Needs a migration or deploy step:** changes stored references, deployed dependencies, backup custody, credentials, or repository history. Plan rollout/rollback and any data changes explicitly.
- **Needs a test:** prove behavior, type compatibility, or test-runner coverage before the approved change. All existing tests and useful assertions must be retained; strengthen weak cases rather than remove them.
- **Safe:** narrowly scoped documentation or unreachable-code cleanup, with the stated verification and owner approval.

Effort is an engineering estimate, excluding review/approval delays. A day means roughly one engineer-day. Related rows overlap; estimates should not simply be added.

## Findings to review first

1. Tracked database backups contain user/session data and credential-related fields. They need a private, tested retention plan; public object storage and Git LFS are not suitable backup custody.
2. The six lockfiles contain **155 distinct advisory URLs affecting 48 package names** (185 affected-version-range records). Several critical advisories concern developer tools; runtime exploitability is a separate question.
3. Current green CI still leaves **28 server `.vitest.ts` files undiscovered**, two real-database suites outside its explicit list, and the end-to-end job disabled. The frontend baseline is **63.19% line coverage**, not a measure of server coverage.
4. A reproducible racial-spell bug was filed as #1994, and the coverage-summary configuration bug as #1995. Neither was fixed.
5. Only **four** remote branch tips are proven ancestors of main. **364** have closed, unmerged PRs and unmatched patches; closure alone is not evidence that their work can be discarded.

## Evidence and limits

Inventory and searches were read-only. Dependencies and audit tools were installed only for local analysis; no lockfile was changed. No production credentials were loaded, no credential was tested against a provider, and no production database or external cron configuration was inspected. Database dumps were processed locally without printing row values.

A TypeScript import graph covered 2,232 tracked JS/TS files, including static imports, re-exports, literal `import()` and `require()`. Separate Knip 5.71.0 scans were used for candidate discovery, first with explicit application/test entry points, then with installed dependencies and standard plugin discovery. These returned 100 and 140 file candidates respectively. **Those lists are not deletion lists.** Only the individually verified findings below are recommendations. HTML/build entries, scripts, ambient declarations, test fixtures, deliberate future work, indirect package loading, and external HTTP clients can all defeat a naive “no imports” claim.

All “unused” statements here mean **no caller/importer in the audited tracked repository**, with the scope and command shown. They do not establish absence of external manual use. Commands run from the repository root unless a working directory is stated. `git grep` exit 1 means no matches, not a failed scan. Re-running after adding this report will also find the report's own text; exclude this report when comparing results.

## 8. Secrets in the tree and history

| S01 · high    | [backups/infiniterealms_20260101_020001.sql.gz](https://github.com/Garblesnarff/infinite-realms-production/blob/ab716f53be7ae8a6de187f60b827e242ceb9991f/backups/infiniterealms_20260101_020001.sql.gz) and all 20 tracked `backups/*.sql.gz`. Archive-aware Gitleaks found 20 `generic-api-key` matches, one per dump. The matched field is `_realtime.extensions.settings.db_password`; sampled values are 44-character encoded strings, consistent with encrypted data, not proof of a usable plaintext password. `public.blog_api_keys` holds 64-character hexadecimal `key_hash` values. Dumps also contain `public.users` and `public.game_sessions` rows. No values are included here. | Keep private. Confirm encryption/key custody and provider state with the owner, then design encrypted object-storage retention and a restore drill. A current credential is **not confirmed**. Any rotation or history rewrite needs explicit owner action. **Needs a migration or deploy step.** | 0.5–1 day investigation; 1–2 days archive/restore work |
| S02 · medium  | [ai-adventure-scribe-main/docs/MIGRATION_GUIDE.md](https://github.com/Garblesnarff/infinite-realms-production/blob/3af1bd3f1d8ca8228318fa98dd80e5264eca1567/ai-adventure-scribe-main/docs/MIGRATION_GUIDE.md) (historical), `docs/SESSION_ARCHIVAL.md`, and `supabase/migrations/README_SESSION_ARCHIVAL.md` at commit `3af1bd3f1d8ca8228318fa98dd80e5264eca1567`. Nine `curl-auth-header` findings were matched back to source and classified as placeholder labels, including a service-role-key label; none is a confirmed live token. See the exact location table below.                                                                                                                 | No rotation justified by these nine placeholders alone. Preserve the finding classification so they are not repeatedly reported as leaks. **Safe** to document; protected Supabase files remain untouched.                                                                                        | 30 minutes                                             |
| S03 · medium  | [.gitleaks.toml](https://github.com/Garblesnarff/infinite-realms-production/blob/ab716f53be7ae8a6de187f60b827e242ceb9991f/.gitleaks.toml), [.github/workflows/ci.yml](https://github.com/Garblesnarff/infinite-realms-production/blob/ab716f53be7ae8a6de187f60b827e242ceb9991f/.github/workflows/ci.yml). History text scan: 2,433 commits / approximately 254 MB; separate archive scan: 169 reachable archive blobs, with 16 hits in unique backup blobs. The current 20 backup paths contain only 16 unique blobs. An ordinary Git diff scan does not inspect compressed binary dump contents.                                                                                             | Retain archive-aware scanning in a separately approved security workflow. Do not interpret a green text-history scan as a clean compressed-backup inventory. **Needs a test.**                                                                                                                    | 0.5 day                                                |

Commands actually run (all scanner outputs were fully redacted and kept outside Git):

```sh
printf '[extend]\nuseDefault = true\n' > /private/tmp/ir-cleanup-audit/gitleaks-default.toml
gitleaks git --config /private/tmp/ir-cleanup-audit/gitleaks-default.toml --log-opts='--all' --redact=100 --ignore-gitleaks-allow --no-banner --report-format json --report-path /private/tmp/ir-cleanup-audit/secrets-history.json .
gitleaks dir --config /private/tmp/ir-cleanup-audit/gitleaks-default.toml --redact=100 --ignore-gitleaks-allow --max-archive-depth 2 --no-banner --report-format json --report-path /private/tmp/ir-cleanup-audit/secrets-tree.json .
git rev-list --objects --all
python3 /private/tmp/ir-cleanup-audit/scan-archives.py
```

The first directory scan ran before installing dependencies or writing the report. The archive script selected reachable `.gz/.zip/.tar/.tgz` blobs from `git rev-list --objects --all`, read each with `git cat-file blob <sha>`, and scanned those local copies with `gitleaks dir --max-archive-depth 3 --redact=100` using the same default-rules configuration. No archives were executed. GitHub-only deleted refs, unreachable objects, encrypted content, external services, runtime environment files, and nonmatching secret formats remain outside what these scans can prove.

## 6. Dependencies

The registry query checked exact resolved versions from all six tracked lockfiles, not just loose version ranges in package manifests. It used the npm [Bulk Advisory API](https://docs.npmjs.com/cli/v11/commands/npm-audit/) and matched each response range using `semver.satisfies`. Only public package names and versions were sent. It did not run `audit fix` or resolve new project versions. Advisory data changes over time; this is a dated snapshot. The complete package/advisory inventory is in Appendix C.

| ID / priority | Evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Recommendation / risk                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Effort                   |
| ------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------ |

The advisory concerns untrusted SQL identifiers. `rg -n 'sql\.identifier\(\|\.as\(' ai-adventure-scribe-main/server-bun/src -g '*.ts'` found two literal `total_count` aliases and no `sql.identifier()` use in that server scan.                                                                                                                                                     | Upgrade and run combat/auth/ownership real-DB regressions. The locked version is affected; this audit **did not establish an exploitable application call**. Do not regenerate or apply migrations in this phase. **Needs a migration or deploy step** for a runtime dependency release.             | 0.5–2 days               |
| P02 · high    | [ai-adventure-scribe-main/bun.lock](https://github.com/Garblesnarff/infinite-realms-production/blob/ab716f53be7ae8a6de187f60b827e242ceb9991f/ai-adventure-scribe-main/bun.lock) locks `sanitize-html@2.17.4`, `react-router-dom@6.30.4`, and `mathjs@14.9.1` in affected ranges. These are closer to application content/routing/dice paths than build-only packages; exact advisory URLs and lock locations are in Appendix C.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Trace attacker-controlled inputs and configuration before assigning application severity; upgrade with content, navigation and dice regressions. **Needs a migration or deploy step.**                                                                                                               | 1–3 days grouped         |
| P03 · high    | [ai-adventure-scribe-main/bun.lock](https://github.com/Garblesnarff/infinite-realms-production/blob/ab716f53be7ae8a6de187f60b827e242ceb9991f/ai-adventure-scribe-main/bun.lock): `vitest@2.1.9` ([critical UI-server advisory](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-5xrq-8626-4rwp)) and `shell-quote@1.8.3` ([critical object-token quoting advisory](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-w7jw-789q-3m8p)). The vendored database lock also has `vitest@3.2.4`, `handlebars@4.7.8`, and `tar@6.2.1/7.4.3` in critical ranges.                                                                                                                                                             | Update the owning toolchains. Vitest requires an exposed UI server for the cited critical scenario; main CI uses `vitest run`. Shell-quote requires attacker-controlled object tokens passed to quoting and then a shell. No such production flow was established here. **Needs a test.**            | 0.5–2 days per toolchain |

Verification commands:

```sh
node /private/tmp/ir-cleanup-audit/dependencies.cjs
# That script parses bun.lock as JSON-with-comments and package-lock.json,
# collecting { packageName: [exactVersions] } from their package records.
# Python urllib.request POSTed that map to:
# https://registry.npmjs.org/-/npm/v1/security/advisories/bulk
node /private/tmp/ir-cleanup-audit/advisories.cjs
node /private/tmp/ir-cleanup-audit/tooling/node_modules/knip/bin/knip.js --directory ai-adventure-scribe-main --reporter json --no-progress
git grep -n -I -E "['\"]pg['\"]|@elysiajs/trpc" -- ':!*.lock' ':!*package-lock.json' ':!prompts/**' ':!**/prompts/**'
```

The scan covered 1,332 package names and 1,819 distinct name/version pairs. An unused-dependency false positive worth preserving: Knip flagged `puppeteer`, but `server-bun/src/services/blog-screenshot-service.ts:1,6,68` imports and launches it. Whether the whole screenshot feature is intended for retirement is a separate decision, not permission to remove the package. Config-loaded packages such as `drizzle-kit`, Vite, Tailwind, and test reporters were also checked rather than accepting initial scanner warnings.

Commands used to inventory test gaps:

```sh
rg -n '\b(it|test|describe)\.(skip|todo|skipIf|runIf)\b' ai-adventure-scribe-main -g '*test*' -g '*spec*'
git ls-files '*test*' '*spec*'
cat ai-adventure-scribe-main/server-bun/scripts/run-isolated-tests.ts
cat ai-adventure-scribe-main/server-bun/vitest.config.ts
cat ai-adventure-scribe-main/vitest.config.ts
cat .github/workflows/ci.yml
python3 /private/tmp/ir-cleanup-audit/inventory.py
XDG_CACHE_HOME=/private/tmp/ir-cleanup-audit/cache gh run view 34288776839 --repo Garblesnarff/infinite-realms-production --log
```

**Verified baseline and local checks:** [main CI run 34288776839](https://github.com/Garblesnarff/infinite-realms-production/actions/runs/34288776839) is successful at the audited SHA: frontend 486 passed files / 2 skipped files, 5,621 passed tests / 74 skipped; ordinary server 721 pass / 104 skip / 0 fail / 0 error across 124 files; real-DB job 63 pass / 0 fail across 13 files. The end-to-end job is skipped. Frontend coverage is 63.19% lines/statements, 82.17% branches, 67.98% functions; this must not be presented as server/combat persistence coverage.

Local Bun 1.3.4 fresh-process server run independently matched **721 pass / 104 skip / 0 fail / 0 error**. No database/provider environment variables were supplied. Local `bun run build` passed, including the client-build secret check; it warned about a large vendor chunk. Four focused Vitest files passed **49 tests**: `TokenService.test.ts`, `TokenService.cross-tab.test.ts`, `combat-action-executor.test.ts`, `spell-validation-extended.test.ts`. Ten repeat runs of those four files also passed. These are baseline checks on unchanged code, not improvements caused by this report. The full frontend suite was verified from exact-SHA CI rather than rerun locally.

The extended racial-spell suite can pass while the actual-data reproduction fails: its selected fixture does not exercise the same subrace data shape. That is why the bug report includes a direct reproduction using real repository race definitions.

## 4. Remote branches

A stale date, `bolt-*`/`guardian-*` name, closed PR, or unmatched patch is not enough to say work is worthless. “Abandoned candidate” below means **closed without merge and no open PR currently using the branch**, not a claim about the owner's intent. Some PRs marked merged have branch tips that differ from their recorded merged head; those are retained for inspection. Squash/rebase/history changes can defeat patch equivalence.

The complete 388-row inventory is Appendix A. Each row includes the exact commit link, last commit date, ahead count, patch-equivalence result where needed, and associated PR(s). `+` means non-merge commits not matched by `git cherry`; it is not a count of useful features. `—` means that check was unnecessary for the category.

Open PRs and their changed paths were fetched before editing.                                     | **Needs a test** for any code integration; keep all three branches. Migration-bearing #1965 also needs its own deploy plan.                               | Existing PR scope; no cleanup action                 |
| B02 · unmerged review       | 16: no qualifying open PR, but not proven incorporated. Includes research archives, deliberately archived work, and tips associated with an earlier merged PR. See preservation shortlist below.                                       | **Needs a test** before integrating code. Keep research/unique tests until owner review.                                                                  | 10–30 min per distinct patch; longer for integration |
| B03 · abandoned candidates  | 364: associated PRs are closed/unmerged; each has one or more unmatched non-merge patches. No safe bulk deletion is supported.                                                                                                         | **Needs a test** / semantic review before treating code as superseded. Preserve unique tests and research. Do not delete branches under Phase 1.          | 2–4 days grouped triage, plus owner decisions        |

Their commits/tests remain reachable from main. | 5–10 min per branch                                  |

No push, merge, deletion or history rewrite authorized.                                                                                             | None                                                 |

Preserve as research; neither code quality nor continued relevance can be inferred from age.                                                                                                           | Safe to retain; 1–2 hours owner curation                                 |

Branch commands actually used:

```sh
git fetch origin  # no prune or branch deletion
gh api --paginate 'repos/Garblesnarff/infinite-realms-production/branches?per_page=100'
gh pr list --repo Garblesnarff/infinite-realms-production --state all --limit 3000 --json number,title,headRefName,baseRefName,state,mergedAt,closedAt,updatedAt,headRefOid,mergeCommit
python3 /private/tmp/ir-cleanup-audit/branches.py
```

For every returned branch tip, that script ran the following read-only checks (using the captured SHA, not a moving branch name):

```sh
git show -s --format=%cs <tip-sha>
git rev-list --count ab716f53be7ae8a6de187f60b827e242ceb9991f..<tip-sha>
git merge-base --is-ancestor <tip-sha> ab716f53be7ae8a6de187f60b827e242ceb9991f
git cherry ab716f53be7ae8a6de187f60b827e242ceb9991f <tip-sha>
git diff --name-only ab716f53be7ae8a6de187f60b827e242ceb9991f...<tip-sha>
```

`git cherry` was run only for tips not already contained or protected by an open PR. The PR comparison also checked exact head SHA, merge-commit ancestry, and open child PR base references. No additional tips qualified as exact merged-head or patch-equivalent. This is conservative: it may retain superseded work, but avoids destroying unmatched work or tests.

No public asset was declared safe to drop based only on a missing source reference. Stored URLs, generated names, external links and deployment behavior have to be checked first.

**Protected from false positives:** `src/blog-client.ts` and `src/landing-client.ts` are Vite entry points (`vite.config.ts:103-104`); `src/lib/stubs/async-hooks.ts` is a Vite alias (`:85`); `src/test/__mocks__/pino.ts` is a test alias (`vitest.config.ts:13`). Ambient `.d.ts` files do not need importers. `server-bun/src/services/dm/dm-response-schema-state-updates.ts:7-13` explicitly documents not-yet-wired groundwork; keep it pending its owning design. `blog-scheduler.ts` has no repository startup call: `git grep -n -I -E 'blog-scheduler|BlogScheduler' -- ':!*.lock' ':!*package-lock.json' ':!docs/audits/**'` found its own implementation and documentation, including a claim that it runs in production. External cron/operational intent was not verified, so its deletion is not recommended.

Route review did not produce a safely removable registered API route. Server routes are registered through `app.ts`/routers and can have clients outside this tree. “No browser call found” is not sufficient route-retirement evidence.

Configuration verification:

```sh
git grep -n -I -E 'VITE_WORKOS_CLIENT_ID|VITE_WORKOS_REDIRECT_URI|VITE_DYNAMIC_OPTIONS|BLOG_SETUP_SECRET' --
git grep -n -I -E 'check-vitest-baseline|known-failures.json' --
git ls-files '*known-failures*'
git grep -n -I -E 'isMultiplayerInvitesEnabled|isCampaignCharacterFlowEnabled|isWorldBuilderEnabled' -- ':!*.lock' ':!*package-lock.json' ':!prompts/**' ':!**/prompts/**'
python3 /private/tmp/ir-cleanup-audit/inventory.py
```

The variable-name inventory found 133 distinct literal access names across scanned source/docs/config, not 133 proven required production variables. No values were reported. `CLI_API_URL`, `CLI_SUPABASE_URL`, `CLI_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, and Discord settings were **not** called unused merely because direct property-access scanning missed them: dynamic environment readers and external MCP launch configuration require separate checks.

## Approval checklist for a later Phase 2

Choose finding IDs and their exact file/branch scope. Keep tests, prompts and migration exclusions intact unless a new agreement explicitly changes the relevant scope. For assets/backups, agree custody and URL/restore evidence first. For branches, preserve unique tests/research and recheck current PR/base relationships. For runtime dependencies, agree regression and deploy checks. Nothing in this report grants approval to implement, delete, merge, deploy, rotate credentials or rewrite history.

Conditional skips are additional: `server-bun/src/services/__tests__/fixtures/real-db.ts` chooses the skipped describe function when its real database is absent. Normal-server 104 and frontend 74 are observed runtime skip counts; they must not be added to 38 source declarations as though these were independent tests. The 13-file real-DB CI job executed 63 passing tests, while solo-survivability and monster-attacks remain outside that list.

## Appendix C — all matched vulnerable package names and advisories

These are advisory matches, not 48 proven application exploits. Maximum severity is the registry's package-advisory severity. Versions, locations and every matched advisory URL are retained below. The same advisory can have several affected version ranges; rows deduplicate those URLs. Lockfile line numbers identify the package record in the audited commit.

Lock abbreviations (each is a file path, not an environment):

- **A** — `ai-adventure-scribe-main/bun.lock` (app + server + CLI).
- **R** — `bun.lock` (root maintenance tools).
- **I** — `ai-adventure-scribe-main/tools/lore-keeper-ingest/package-lock.json`.
- **D** — `dnd-5e-mcp-server/package-lock.json`.
- **V** — `dnd-5e-mcp-server/data/5e-database/package-lock.json` (vendored maintenance tooling).
- **L** — `lore-keeper-mcp-server/package-lock.json`.

Runtime upgrades need a deploy step; tooling-only upgrades need tests. Estimates in this appendix are grouped package triage/upgrade estimates, not additive commitments.

| Package                   | Affected locked versions            | Max severity | Lock paths/lines ## Appendix D — import-graph method and reproducibility

Original execution: `node /private/tmp/ir-cleanup-audit/graph.cjs`, followed by the D01–D08 exact searches above. The graph parsed tracked JS/TS files with the TypeScript compiler API and resolved modules using bundler resolution plus the app's `@/*` alias. It checked incoming imports independently of reachability from the main app/server/CLI/MCP roots. Five non-literal dynamic imports were found in combat intent code/tests and reviewed as exceptions; the graph alone cannot decide their targets.

A portable equivalent of the incoming-edge check is below. It reads files only; run it from the repo root after installing the existing app dependencies. It is deliberately a screening tool: no-import entry points, ambient declarations and file-system loading must still be checked manually. This code is documentation inside this one report, not an installed repo tool.

```js
const fs = require("node:fs");
const path = require("node:path");
const cp = require("node:child_process");
const ts = require("./ai-adventure-scribe-main/node_modules/typescript");
const root = process.cwd();
const files = cp
  .execFileSync("git", ["ls-files", "-z"], { encoding: "utf8" })
  .split("\0")
  .filter((f) => /\.(?:[cm]?[jt]sx?)$/.test(f));
const incoming = new Map(files.map((f) => [f, []]));
const options = {
  moduleResolution: ts.ModuleResolutionKind.Bundler,
  allowJs: true,
  baseUrl: path.join(root, "ai-adventure-scribe-main"),
  paths: { "@/*": ["src/*"], "@db/*": ["db/*"] },
};
for (const file of files) {
  const source = ts.createSourceFile(
    file,
    fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8"),
    ts.ScriptTarget.Latest,
    true,
  );
  function spec(node) {
    if (!node || !ts.isStringLiteralLike(node)) return;
    const resolved = ts.resolveModuleName(
      node.text,
      path.join(root, file),
      options,
      ts.sys,
    ).resolvedModule;
    const target = resolved && path.relative(root, resolved.resolvedFileName);
    if (incoming.has(target)) incoming.get(target).push(file);
  }
  function visit(node) {
    if (ts.isImportDeclaration(node) || ts.isExportDeclaration(node))
      spec(node.moduleSpecifier);
    if (
      ts.isCallExpression(node) &&
      (node.expression.kind === ts.SyntaxKind.ImportKeyword ||
        (ts.isIdentifier(node.expression) &&
          node.expression.text === "require"))
    )
      spec(node.arguments[0]);
    ts.forEachChild(node, visit);
  }
  visit(source);
}
for (const [file, refs] of incoming) if (refs.length === 0) console.log(file);
```

The secondary configured Knip scan treated source tests, scripts, `src/main.tsx`, `src/blog-client.ts`, `src/landing-client.ts`, server `src/index.ts`, server `.test.ts`/`.vitest.ts` files, and CLI entry/tests as roots. Config plugins were disabled only for that preliminary scan because dependencies were not yet installed; the later default scan had dependencies available. Neither scan changed files. File candidates whose status depended on those configuration differences were not presented as proven dead code.
